La réserve renverse Le Cannet-Rocheville sous les yeux de Lopez

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sous les yeux de Christian Lopez, qui a donné le coup d'envoi fictif de cette rencontre entre ses deux anciens clubs, la réserve s'est imposée (2-1) ce samedi sur le fil et sur le terrain du Cannet-Rocheville (Alpes Maritimes) grâce à renversant doublé de Lamine Sonko (photo).

Après un premier quart d'heure plutôt équilibré, l'ASSE a pris l'ascendant mais n'a pas su ouvrir le score malgré deux grosses occasions de son très remuant ailier gauche Etienne Mendy et une tentative de Mamadou Konté sauvée in extremis sur sa ligne par un défenseur.

Sainté a concédé l'ouverture du score à la 56e minute suite à un 6 mètres joué trop vite suivi d'une perte de balle. Les Maralpins ont bien joué le coup sur cette action qui semblait quand même entachée d'une légère faute sur Mamadou Konté.

Boostés par l'apport de leurs entrants, les Verts ont égalisé à la 86e minute sur une ouverture de leur latéral droit Tom Teillol pour Lamine Sonko, qui a résisté au retour de deux défenseurs avant de mettre un petit piqué pour tromper le mal prénommé mais bien nommé Victor Battu.

A la 92e minute, Nathan Kasia a perdu le ballon mais s'est bagarré pour le récupérer et lancer Noah Moulin, dont le centre tendu a été repris victorieusement par le supersub Lamine Sonko. Ce succès permet aux Verts de monter provisoirement à la 2e place du classement, derrière le solide leader Berre.

La réaction de Sylvain Gibert : "On a su faire la différence à la fin du match mais on aurait mérité de mener au score à la pause car la dernière demi-heure de la première période a été très bonne. On a manqué d'efficacité devant le but, c'est donc notre adversaire qui a ouvert le score. Mais on est reparti de l'avant, on a insisté et au final on a été récompensé. C'est la victoire de tout un groupe, les entrants notamment nous ont apporté l'énergie nécessaire pour renverser ce match. Les joueus étaient venus ici pour gagner et ils se sont donnés les moyens d'y parvenir. Il faut les féliciter pour ça. On est content d'avoir décroché ce succès sous les yeux de Christian Lopez, qui nous a fait l'honneur d'être présent et d'échanger avec nous à la fin du match. C'était top car c'est un joueur que j'ai cité la saison dernière dans mes causeries. Je trouve qu'il représente les valeurs du club, cette capacité à ne jamais renoncer, comme l'illustre son mythique intervention sur Oleg Blokhine contre Kiev."

Man City's slump cannot be excused by guilty verdict: Too many blunders, too much arm waving and football problems are piling up after 3-1 defeat by Nottingham Forest, writes JACK GAUGHAN

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:52 EDT, 7 November 2026 | Updated: 15:31 EDT, 7 November 2026

This run will not be about the verdicts. This cannot be about the verdicts. Footballers do not care about these things, the stuff out of their control. Besides, all of them are earning cut-price transfers elsewhere were Manchester City to suffer relegation anyway. And cut-price fees mean bulkier salaries.

So to them the current climate, the circumstances around City – their recent history and their immediate future – does not overly matter. Sure, there might be the odd one who is slightly more philosophical and inquisitive about appeals and appendices but the majority won't be giving it much or any thought.

City insist on their innocence publicly and privately; until that changes, their players and Enzo Maresca carry on regardless. There were the usual jeers here, there were ditties about cheating, but that surely cannot affect them.

Instead, the performances over the past few weeks, this a third consecutive Premier League defeat and now sinking six points adrift of Arsenal, cannot be excused by the findings of an independent commission. The problems are football problems.

This is a squad who appear uncertain about specific aspects of their game. Namely how to build from the back – and that is prompting individual mistakes. It happened against Ipswich Town and at Aston Villa. On the river Trent, it was no different.

The statistics say City dominated another game and they deserved more but the eyes tell another story.

Man City are a squad who now appear uncertain about specific aspects of their game

The statistics say City dominated another game and they deserved more but the eyes tell another story

Three defeats in a row have seen Enzo Maresca's side slip six points behind leaders Arsenal

Too many errors. Too much arm waving. Too much feeling sorry for themselves when decisions go elsewhere. Oliver Glasner is not stupid, he will have seen examples of this play out in parts of City's victories as well as reverses.

As Liam Delap raced into the corner to celebrate his second with 17 minutes left – a huge day in his career against the club which moulded him – Glasner was dashing down the touchline with unbridled joy.

Nottingham Forest (5-2-2-1): Sels 6; Munoz 6 (Netz 65, 6), Cunha 8, Milenkovic 8, Murillo 7, Williams 8; Yates 8 (Sangare 79), Schlager 8; McAtee 7 (Ndoye 65, 6), Gibbs-White 7; Delap 9 (Jesus 87)

Man City (4-1-4-1): Donnarumma 5; Khusanov 5, Dias 5, Guehi 4 (Nunes 46, 7), Gvardiol 4 (O'Reilly 46, 6); Anderson 5; Ndiaye 6, Cherki 6, Fernandez 4 (Foden 78), Doku 6; Haaland 5

Forest displays have deserved more than their points total so far and this was vindication for their fifth manager in 14 months. Delap was exceptional, some personal redemption after criticism.

The way in which Glasner nudged James McAtee further forward, stationed higher from the off in order to pressurise the visitors, spoke to an idea that City arrived here ready to be ruffled and have been wearing a look of fragility.

By the time this was eight minutes shy of half time and McAtee was nicking possession from Elliot Anderson, drawing a foul that sparked another onslaught on Gianluigi Donnarumma's box, Forest were already two clear – and had been for some time. Phil Foden warmed up on the touchline, laughing and hugging with Callum Hudson-Odoi.

Forest preyed on City insecurities. A midfield structure that is simply not working at the moment, easily railroaded. A defence allergic to the basics and an epidemic of individual mistakes – either as a result of nervousness or blase attitude on the ball.

Straight to the mistakes. A real collection, these: the first in the fifth minute and the others 13 minutes afterwards. Jair Cunha wheeled away after picking Josko Gvardiol's pocket, meandering forward into the box and side-footing into the far corner largely unchallenged. Gvardiol seemed to have the air of a man who felt invincible when gathering possession before being eased off it.

Later, as Delap thundered forward 45 yards unchecked to hammer past Donnarumma, you wondered about the communication between these City players. Anderson and Iliman Ndiaye left things to each other near halfway, Xaver Schlager gave his thanks – finding Delap – and the City academy graduate bulldozed clear. Marc Guehi waved him through, Ruben Dias dithered and Maresca bowed his head.

Liam Delap was exceptional for Nottingham Forest, some personal redemption after criticism

Remarkably, this was the first time in seven years that City had gone two down inside 18 minutes

Remarkably, this was the first time in seven years that City had gone two down inside 18 minutes. Dias looked aghast at Guehi, who was then hooked at the break alongside Gvardiol. Erling Haaland and Rayan Cherki both stood in the centre circle, hands on hips.

It later emerged via replays that Anderson had been arguing with Abdukodir Khusanov in the build-up to Forest's opener as well, proof that City were nowhere near close to producing what was expected when on the ball in their own half.

Glasner had the weapons at his disposal to make this work but even the Austrian must have been surprised at the way in which they tickled City's soft underbelly. They had not scored at the City Ground all season. All change when owner Evangelos Marinakis made his first trip to see a game since Glasner's appointment, which felt particularly handy.

Anger permeated the eyes of some City players as the whistle went. Cherki stormed down the tunnel, animatedly discussing how to play through Forest with Fernandez when they re-emerged.

Their lifeline came after 52 minutes, a Ryan Yates deflection from Jeremy Doku's cross sitting up for Cherki to hammer home a half-volley off the bar. Doku soon almost squeezed another effort past Matz Sels and the mood shifted, City briefly creeping menacingly like the monsters they are portrayed as.

Maresca had introduced Matheus Nunes and Nico O'Reilly, with both affecting the upturn. An admission of selection errors, perhaps, although O'Reilly is managing an ongoing back issue and is set for therapy on it over the international break.

City will need some of that as a collective. Delap won the ball inside his own third, gliding past Anderson, and Forest's explosive counter ended with Delap spearing a shot in off the post after Morgan Gibbs-White carried possession unopposed for 60 yards. Delirium, Glasner darting to the corner flag.

The quirk of this is that City's failings mean Manchester United drop into the bottom three.

Magnus Ditlev Sets New Iron-Distance World Best With 7:19:17 at Ironman Florida

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Magnus Ditlev set the fastest-ever Iron-distance time at the 2026 Ironman Florida (Photo by Sean M. Haffey/Getty Images for IRONMAN) (Photo: Getty Images for IRONMAN)

One month after a heartbreaking DNF in Kona, Magnus Ditlev (DEN) rode away from the field at Ironman Florida and never looked back. His 7:19:17 took 1:47 off the previous men’s iron-distance world best that Sam Laidlow (FRA) set at Challenge Roth earlier this year.

Florida was a chance at redemption for Ditlev, who had come agonizingly close to winning the Ironman World Championship in Kona. He’d taken the lead late on the bike and looked on course for the win until he abruptly came to a stop on the run with roughly 30 minutes of racing left.

However, ahead of Kona, Ditlev said he was putting up career-best numbers across the swim, bike, and run at his training camp in Colorado. In Florida, he finally got to reap the rewards of what fitness could produce all the way to the finish line.

Ditlev came out of the water in fifth, right on the heels of Ben Kanute (USA) and Rudy von Berg (USA). He took his time in T1 and started the bike nearly a minute down, but it didn’t take him long to ride back to the front. And then, just past mile 10, he made his move. No one could go with him, and they wouldn’t be able to stay remotely close to him either.

By the time Ditlev came off the bike, he was 11 minutes clear of a chase group that included Sam Long (USA), von Berg, and Kanute. His 3:51:16 was the fastest bike split ever recorded at an Ironman, nearly two minutes quicker than the 3:53:12 Robert Kallin (SWE) rode at Ironman Kalmar in August.

Off the bike, he once again took his time in transition. But transitions were the only slow part of Ditlev’s day. Once through T2, he picked up another minute on the chasers over the opening 10 miles. He hit halfway in 1:15, on pace for a 2:30 marathon and well within reach of Laidlow’s 7:21:04. With such an unassailable lead, one can assume he must’ve known the record was within reach to keep such an aggressive pace.

Ditlev eventually closed with a 2:33:31 marathon, crossing the line in 7:19:17. Von Berg took second, 14 minutes back, securing the early Kona slot and podium he’d come for. Matt Hanson (USA), meanwhile, ran a remarkable 2:28:07 to beat Long to the final podium spot, but still finished 16 minutes behind Ditlev.

"This trailer is my way of saying goodbye": Have a listen to the Mass Effect 5 score that could have been, courtesy of one of the original games' composers

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

By this point I'm probably safe in assuming that you all know EA are shutting down BioWare, and that Mass Effect 5 has been cancelled in turn. You may have also seen that an internal trailer for Mass Effect 5, or Universe as it was seemingly going to be called, was leaked not long after the news broke, and that Sam Hulick, one of the composers on the original trilogy of games, shared he was going to offer up his re-scored version of the trailer. Well, it's N7 Day, as rough as that will be for many of the developers on the game, but Hulick has stood by his word, and shared an oral glimpse at what the world of Mass Effect Universe could have sounded like.

You can check out this new version of the trailer below, perhaps pop it on while you have a read of the rest of this post, as Hulick did also write in a Patreon blog about his experience with the series. "As part of my own closure, and as a love letter to the fans, I really wanted to re-score the leaked Mass Effect Universe trailer in time for N7 Day this year," he wrote. "BioWare edited a killer track called “Quartz” by French 79 to fit their trailer, which I think hinted at a slight directional shift in the score. Using that as inspiration, along with the musical tone from my work on the first three Mass Effect games, I hit the studio and got to work."

As it turns out, BioWare had actually reached out to Hulick asking him to potentially do some work for the project. "This year, BioWare emailed me asking if I was interested in working with them on an internal demo, and they said they were 'hoping to get some music written that would feel like Mass [Effect], supporting our new direction.' Of course, I said yes!" This doesn't mean he wouldn't have composed the actual game, as a composer hadn't been officially selected, but Hulick said he was "more than happy to collaborate on the new game’s musical direction and help make their internal demo sound as good as it could."

Hulick also spoke of some of the ideas he had for the direction Mass Effect Universe's soundtrack could take, noting how he "thought it would’ve been incredible to bring more songs with lyrics into the game," considering how many people loved the first game's end-credits song. "This would’ve been a great way to highlight more emotional moments in the narrative. Artists at the top of my collaboration list included Gunship, NINA, and The Midnight, all of whom have a moody, cinematic synthwave vibe that would’ve meshed well with the score in my head. And I’m pretty sure the Gunship guys are gamers, and if they’re Mass Effect fans, that would’ve been the icing on the cake!"

Of course, with the studio now closing, this never got to go very far, the news hitting Hulick "particularly hard, as the Mass Effect trilogy is a core part of my origin story as a composer." For the composer, ultimately "this trailer is my way of saying goodbye… at least for now." This continues to be a horrendously sad moment in the industry! As always, solidarity with the team at BioWare.

Advantage Arsenal in Premier League title race as Man City stunned by Nottingham Forest

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Liam Delap’s emphatic brace earned Nottingham Forest a first home win of the season and piled the misery on Manchester City with a 3-1 victory.

Delap scored in either half after Jair Cunha had given Forest an early lead as City lost for the third Premier League game in a row.

This victory could be a coming of age for both Delap and Forest, who had struggled to adapt to life under new boss Oliver Glasner, with just one win from their opening nine games.

Delap had scored once in his opening eight games following his club-record move from Chelsea, but his brilliant double proved he can be the man to fire Forest to better things this season.

The win is their first in the league at the City Ground since April and sends Manchester United into the bottom three.

City found themselves 2-0 down inside 20 minutes, but did threaten a comeback when Rayan Cherki scored early in the second half.

But they slipped to a third loss on the bounce and amid the backdrop of the ongoing appeal against Premier League charges, their season is showing signs of falling apart.

Forest enjoyed an opening 20 minutes they could never have imagined as they were all over City and surged into a two-goal lead.

Having waited the best part of eight and a half hours to score in front of their fans, perhaps unsurprisingly the drought, going back to last May, was ended by an unlikely source in the sixth minute.

Centre-back Jair robbed Josko Gvardiol high up the pitch, advanced into the area and slotted past Gianluigi Donnarumma.

It was 2-0 in the 19th minute as Delap broke his City Ground duck in fine style.

Former Forest midfielder Elliot Anderson, who had a nightmare return to the ground where he turned into a £116million player, and Iliman Ndiaye left the ball to each other in the Forest half and Delap pounced, running all the way into the City area before firing an unstoppable shot past Donnarumma.

Arteta responds to Arsenal penalty decision amid VAR controversy

Discover a hidden island paradise in the Indian Ocean

James McAtee almost made it three soon after, but he skied an effort from 16 yards after Forest had again robbed City of possession.

The visitors were rattled and had a big repair job on their hands.

They should have got one back in the 25th minute as Marc Guehi’s cross was perfect for Enzo Fernandez, who could not keep his header from six yards down.

Erling Haaland had been a virtual spectator but came alive in the 40th minute, only to be denied by some great defending by Jair.

Anderson’s ball to Abdukodir Khusanov split the Forest defence and the right-back fed Haaland, who rounded goalkeeper Matz Sels but saw his shot heroically cleared off the line by Jair.

But they did get one back in the 52nd minute as Cherki delivered a firecracker.

Jeremy Doku was clear down the left and his cross was deflected into the path of the France international, who slammed a half-volley into the top corner from the edge of the area.

City smelled blood and Haaland was a whisker away from levelling when his curled effort from 12 yards went just over.

Forest appeared to have weathered the storm and could have regained their two-goal advantage midway through the second half.

Fernandez was dispossessed and Ryan Yates’ shot cannoned off Anderson and looped on to the top of the crossbar.

But they did make it 3-1 with a brilliant breakaway goal in the 73rd minute.

Delap set Morgan Gibbs-White free in their own half and they raced the length of the pitch, with the England international returning the ball to the striker, who sent an emphatic finishing crashing in off the post.

That was enough to get them over the line as they ended a five-game winless run.

Southwest Airlines Flight Attendant Allegedly Quit Job After Passenger-Facing Crew Dispute

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

A Southwest Airlines flight attendant allegedly quit after a verbal argument with other cabin crew in front of a fully boarded plane. A report by View From The Wing has suggested that passengers were told a flight attendant was sick before the captain of the flight to Kansas City came on the loudspeaker and explained that the two had 'personality differences'.

Just this past Friday, Southwest flight 557 was set to operate from Dallas Love Field (DAL) to Kansas City International Airport (MCI), when the pushback of the jet was delayed due to the disagreement between the two co-workers. The argument set the flight back several hours.

Shared on a passenger's Reddit page, an eyewitness passenger onboard described the argument between the two Southwest flight attendants taking place at the front of the aircraft in full view for all of the passengers onboard. This resulted in the crew being taken off the aircraft and then it being blamed on them being sick.

This performance in front of the other passengers forced the captain to speak to the passengers onboard to explain the situation, with the blame being put down to personality differences. Further claims have suggested that the flight was already delayed by ten minutes, but the full delay of up to two hours before the jet finally pushed back and departed for Kansas City was down to the crew dispute.

Simple Flying reached out to Southwest for comment on Saturday; however, an airline spokesperson could not immediately be reached.

As suggested by VFTW, one of the Southwest flight attendants involved in the argument has since quit their job with the world's largest operator of the Boeing 737, after they were embarrassed after having a verbal argument with another crew member in front of a fully boarded aircraft.

Southwest flight 557, a regular scheduled six-weekly service between Dallas and Kansas City, was scheduled to depart at 2:25 PM. Due to an initial maintenance delay, and then the subsequent crew argument, the flight was delayed two hours and 24 minutes before the aircraft eventually took off.

The short one-hour, four-minute journey then landed in Kansas City at 6:16 PM that evening, two hours and 21 minutes behind its initial scheduled arrival time of 3:55 PM. The aircraft operating the service was that of a Boeing 737-700, bearing registration N255WN. Full aircraft characteristics are in the table below, as per ch-aviation:

N281WN the carrier's 500th 737 circled over the South Bay as pilots ran emergency checklists and prepared for a single‑engine return.

With the crew member allegedly quitting and refusing to operate the flight, this ultimately forced Southwest to find a replacement crew member to crew the service to Kansas City, and was the primary cause of the flight's delay.

To crew a 737-700, the typical requirements are two pilots and three cabin crew to serve the total of 137 passengers in an all-economy class configuration. Southwest is the largest operator of the 737-700, with more than 270 active aircraft in its fleet.

Middlesbrough, QPR & West Brom have all dodged Bamba Dieng transfer bullet

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Middlesbrough, Queens Park Rangers, and West Bromwich Albion were all linked with Senegal international Bamba Dieng during the summer transfer window, but none ultimately pursued a deal.

The 26-year-old instead joined Serbian giants Red Star Belgrade on a free transfer, where he has enjoyed some encouraging moments in front of goal after not joining Middlesbrough, QPR, or West Brom.

However, there is a growing argument that the Championship trio might have been wise to steer clear, particularly when looking beyond Bamba Dieng's domestic scoring record and assessing how he has performed on the European stage.

Dieng has certainly found the net a reasonable number of times since arriving in Serbia, but there is a significant difference between scoring in the Serbian SuperLiga and delivering consistently in the Championship.

More importantly, his performances in the UEFA Conference League have hardly suggested he would have been a transformative signing for the likes of Middlesbrough, West Brom, or QPR this season. Boro were even in talks with Dieng at one stage of the transfer window.

All three clubs should arguably be targeting promotion and, given their size and stature, would surely fancy their chances of competing for glory in a European competition of that nature if they were playing at that level.

FLW have ranked all 24 Championship clubs on how far they have fallen from their historical glory days, including Blackburn and West Ham.

A high-wage arrival such as Dieng should be demonstrating genuine upside against continental opposition, rather than simply padding his statistics and goal involvements in domestic fixtures for a club the size and stature of Red Star Belgrade in Serbia's top flight.

Red Star have faced Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes, with that trio being opponents that Championship sides would reasonably expect to beat or at least give a very good account of themselves against.

Dieng has failed to register a goal or assist in any of those three matches. Perhaps all three English clubs had the right idea by ultimately looking elsewhere or sticking with what they had in the end. The fact he limped off the pitch assisted by medial staff in their most recent fixture underlines this, too.

Dieng began his professional career in Senegal with Diambars before earning a move to Marseille in 2021, where he scored eight goals in 40 appearances. He subsequently joined Lorient permanently in January 2023, spending time on loan at Angers before enjoying his most productive campaign yet in 2025/26.

The 24-cap Senegal international may well have made the move to the Championship over the recent summer but he has also experienced missing out on a Premier League move, having agreed to join Leeds United from Marseille in September 2022 before a dramatic deadline-day change of plans saw Nice attempt to hijack the deal (BBC Sport).

Are you happy with your club's striking options with January just around the corner? Comment below!

Since joining Red Star on a free transfer in August, he has scored five goals in seven Serbian league appearances. However, he has no goals or assists in three Conference League outings, totalling just over 200 minutes, against opposition of the stature and quality of Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes.

Dieng would not have been somebody who could comfortably arrive as an expensive substitute at any of these three clubs, meaning his signing would have carried significant consequences for their existing attacking options.

At Middlesbrough, he could have been brought in instead of Will Lankshear, which would have been a considerable mistake given the former Tottenham Hotspur youngster's extraordinary goalscoring form in the Championship.

Type the top scorer next to each season. Surnames count. Two seasons had joint winners, so they need two names.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

QPR might have been tempted to install him ahead of Richard Kone, potentially stunting the development of one of their most exciting young attackers. Meanwhile, West Brom already have Jimmy-Jay Morgan performing well, with Aune Heggebo also contributing positively.

All three clubs have younger forwards worth developing rather than blocking with an expensive, high-wage alternative. Dieng might yet prove to be a success in Serbia, but the Championship trio arguably made the right decisions for their respective squads.

Mass Effect 3 Legendary Edition Mod Restores Multiplayer

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Mass Effect 3’s criminally underrated multiplayer mode has been restored and added to the Legendary Edition as a special N7 Day treat from a skilled group of fans.

Today, November 7, should be a very special occasion for Mass Effect fans. Back when the series was alive and thriving, BioWare coined N7 Day as a time to celebrate the series and share updates about its future. Of course, with BioWare and the next big game in the series, Universe, recently being shut down by EA, the atmosphere is a little dire.

As the leaked trailer for Universe and all of the new merchandise have kept proving, BioWare had some big plans in store for this year’s N7 Day. Even though the future of Mass Effect is a complete unknown and everyone is still in mourning, fans have come together to try and give the series a worthy send-off, which an impressive modding project has certainly done.

As pointed out by ResetEra user Lord Vatek, renowned Mass Effect modding group ME3Tweaks decided to cheer us all up on this rather depressing N7 Day by releasing Legendary Edition Multiplayer. The mod, which is currently live on Nexus Mods, is a “full game conversion” that adds Mass Effect 3’s multiplayer mode to the remaster.

Although Mass Effect 3’s multiplayer was a little divisive at launch, it’s gone on to be a bit of a cult classic for fans and was considered a notable omission from the Legendary Edition. It’s something that’s still talked about to this day, making its comeback more than five years later a pretty big deal. Especially considering it’s N7 Day.

The mod doesn’t just recreate the original multiplayer, though, as ME3Tweaks actually built on it in some pretty big ways. Heavy weapons like the Reaper Blackstar and Cain are now available, as are an overhauled photo mode, configurable aim assist, and improved FOV settings. New banners, accessibility options, and improved textures round everything out as an impressive expansion of the original mode.

As if all of that isn’t enough, ME3Tweaks aims to add matchmaking server statistics, a proper multiplayer store, and a total rework of the challenges screen.

There are already plenty of impressive Mass Effect mods out there, including some of this modding team’s previous work, but Legendary Edition Multiplayer has to be up there as one of the best. It’s a damn good way of showing just how passionate the Mass Effect community is, even as the series gets put through hell and back.

We opened 49 packs of the new set, this is what we thought.

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Liam Delap’s emphatic brace earned Nottingham Forest a first home win of the season and piled the misery on Manchester City with a 3-1 victory.

Delap scored in either half after Jair Cunha had given Forest an early lead as City lost for the third Premier League game in a row.

This victory could be a coming of age for both Delap and Forest, who had struggled to adapt to life under new boss Oliver Glasner, with just one win from their opening nine games.

Delap had scored once in his opening eight games following his club-record move from Chelsea, but his brilliant double proved he can be the man to fire Forest to better things this season.

The win is their first in the league at the City Ground since April and sends Manchester United into the bottom three.

City found themselves 2-0 down inside 20 minutes, but did threaten a comeback when Rayan Cherki scored early in the second half.

But they slipped to a third loss on the bounce and amid the backdrop of the ongoing appeal against Premier League charges, their season is showing signs of falling apart.

Forest enjoyed an opening 20 minutes they could never have imagined as they were all over City and surged into a two-goal lead.

Having waited the best part of eight and a half hours to score in front of their fans, perhaps unsurprisingly the drought, going back to last May, was ended by an unlikely source in the sixth minute.

Centre-back Jair robbed Josko Gvardiol high up the pitch, advanced into the area and slotted past Gianluigi Donnarumma.

It was 2-0 in the 19th minute as Delap broke his City Ground duck in fine style.

Former Forest midfielder Elliot Anderson, who had a nightmare return to the ground where he turned into a £116m player, and Iliman Ndiaye left the ball to each other in the Forest half and Delap pounced, running all the way into the City area before firing an unstoppable shot past Donnarumma.

James McAtee almost made it three soon after, but he skied an effort from 16 yards after Forest had again robbed City of possession.

The visitors were rattled and had a big repair job on their hands.

They should have got one back in the 25th minute as Marc Guehi’s cross was perfect for Enzo Fernandez, who could not keep his header from six yards down.

Erling Haaland had been a virtual spectator but came alive in the 40th minute, only to be denied by some great defending by Jair.

Anderson’s ball to Abdukodir Khusanov split the Forest defence and the right-back fed Haaland, who rounded goalkeeper Matz Sels but saw his shot heroically cleared off the line by Jair.

But they did get one back in the 52nd minute as Cherki delivered a firecracker.

Jeremy Doku was clear down the left and his cross was deflected into the path of the France international, who slammed a half-volley into the top corner from the edge of the area.

City smelled blood and Haaland was a whisker away from levelling when his curled effort from 12 yards went just over.

Forest appeared to have weathered the storm and could have regained their two-goal advantage midway through the second half.

Fernandez was dispossessed and Ryan Yates’ shot cannoned off Anderson and looped on to the top of the crossbar.

But they did make it 3-1 with a brilliant breakaway goal in the 73rd minute.

Delap set Morgan Gibbs-White free in their own half and they raced the length of the pitch, with the England international returning the ball to the striker, who sent an emphatic finishing crashing in off the post.

That was enough to get them over the line as they ended a five-game winless run.

Reba McEntire Reveals Dolly Parton’s Touching On-Set Gesture: ‘She Didn’t Have To Do That’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Reba McEntire was determined to make Dolly Parton feel welcome when the country legend joined her sitcom for a guest appearance—but it was Parton who ended up making everyone on-set feel special.

While speaking at NAR NXT in New Orleans alongside her manager and former real estate agent, Justin McIntosh, McEntire recalled the thoughtful gesture Parton made for every crew member behind the scenes of her hit sitcom “Reba”—a small act of kindness that left a lasting impression.

Instead of simply showing up and delivering her lines, Parton made a point of personally greeting the people working behind the scenes, bringing along Valentine’s Day candy and a message for every crew member.

For McEntire, the gesture remains a vivid example of the warmth that made her fellow singer so beloved.

“I was so nervous with Dolly being on the set. I wanted everyone to be nice. What I didn’t expect was how nice she was going to be on the set," she said.

McEntire recalled that Parton took the time to make each person feel appreciated, turning a guest appearance into a chance to connect with the crew.

“She had Valentine’s Day candies, and she went to every crew member individually and said how happy she was to be there,” she said. “She didn’t have to do that.”

The memory has stayed with McEntire, along with a conversation the two shared while sitting together on the iconic show's couch.

“I asked her if she would ever retire,” McEntire recalled. “And she said, ‘What would I do that I could have so much fun doing?’”

It was a simple answer, but one that reflected the enthusiasm McEntire remembers so fondly.

“I’ve never met a person or known a person that’s so universally loved like Dolly Parton,” she said. “I’ve never heard anything bad about her. She was that special of a person.”

That sense of gratitude for a career filled with opportunities to connect with people is something McEntire shares.

“I’m just really grateful and thankful for doing what I do at the level I do,” she said.

For McEntire, keeping her work exciting means giving herself room to try new things—and inviting her audience along for the experience.

“When I change things up, my fans stay interested with me,” she explained. “If it looks like I’m having fun, then they’re having fun.”

The actress revealed that being able to enjoy her work is a gift.

“If you are doing what you love, then that’s how blessed you are,” she said. “That’s one of the greatest gifts God can give, to give you a job you love. I’m very grateful for my job.”

The connection she has built with her audience also rests on the principle that people can recognize sincerity principle—something she believes applies well beyond entertainment.

“I try to sing the songs from my heart so when you listen to it, it touches your heart,” she said.

That emphasis on warmth and connection carries into the way McEntire searches for a home, too.

Long before her career brought her to stages and television sets, she grew up on an 8,000-acre ranch in Oklahoma.

Despite the expanse of land outside, her family of six shared a small house with three bedrooms and one bathroom.

Asked what she first thinks of when she remembers the ranch, McEntire named the people and feelings that filled it.

“Love, family, unity, closeness,” she said. “One itty-bitty bathroom, three bedrooms. We were a close family.”

Gathering around the dinner table was part of life, and the atmosphere of the house remains easy for her to recall.

“We all sat at the dinner table together,” she said. “It was earthy. I can still smell how the house smelled and the energy.”

The land remains a family home. McEntire says it stayed in the family after her parents died, and her sister and her sister’s family now live on the property.

Those early memories offer a glimpse of what McEntire still values when she walks into a house: a feeling of comfort and a connection that goes beyond its appearance.

“A house has energy, and when you come in and when you bond with that energy, it makes it a home,” she said. “So I always look for good, positive energy when I come in.”

The country icon reflected on the moment she experienced that pull while looking for property in Tennessee.

A friend who was vacationing in Florida with a real estate agent spotted a listing the agent was viewing and urged that it be shown to McEntire.

“My friend said, ‘You gotta show Reba that place,’” she recalled.

The approach to the property and a creek at the back helped capture her attention. Soon, she was returning for visits and spending time with the owners.

“The love I had for that house, it took my breath away,” she said. “I went a couple times, and I watched football games with the owners.”

When it comes to her priorities in a home, being comfortable and enjoying the surroundings rank high.

“I love a home with a view,” she said. “When I lived in Oklahoma, you could see sunrise and sunset. That’s important to me.”

McEntire said that focus on people and their needs also helped draw her to her partnership with Realtor.com®.

“What y'all do is very important, you care so much, that's why I like Realtor.com. I love your organization because you have heart...You really do dive into the family and what their needs are. You love people and you care about people, that's why I wanted to be with you guys," she said.

Whether she is discussing finding a home or maintaining the trust of an audience, McEntire returns to the importance of treating people well—and continuing to do so over time.

“You have to stay consistently honest, because when you look someone in the eye, they know how you treated them last time,” she said. “You have to stay consistent with it.”

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

The wild Premier League table since Man City’s charges were announced – hand the trophy to Arsenal now?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

It looks increasingly as though the Premier League could be a cakewalk for the reigning champions Arsenal – whether or not Manchester City are handed a points deduction.

Man City went into the last international break with a 100% winning record under new manager Enzo Maresca, with it all looking like business as usual following the departure of their era-defining manager Pep Guardiola at the end of last season.

However, things have taken a severe dip after the news broke that the club have been found guilty of the vast majority of their 115 charges.

The Athletic’s David Ornstein originally broke the news over the extended international break, and a few days later the Premier League confirmed it with a statement that could have massive repercussions for the English top flight.

“An independent Commission has found Manchester City FC guilty of all charges related to serious breaches of the Premier League’s financial rules over a nine-season period, and the majority of charges in relation to its failure to co-operate with the League’s investigation,” read their statement.

Any potential sanctions are yet to be confirmed or announced, while Man City have lodged an appeal against the verdict, having stated that they’re “both disappointed and surprised by the opinion of the Premier League Commission” and continuing to protest their innocence.

City responded in a defiant mood, having extended their winning run by going to Anfield and beating Liverpool 3-0. But a few days later, they were defeated 4-2 to PSG in the Champions League and a serious domestic slump has followed.

Maresca’s men have now suffered three successive Premier League defeats, a run that started with a 2-1 defeat away to Aston Villa. Losses to Brighton and Nottingham Forest have followed.

The result leaves them six points behind Arsenal at this fledgling stage of the Premier League campaign. The Gunners have won nine of their 10 league outings, are consistently keeping clean sheets, and the recent form is ominous.

The question of a points deduction when it comes to a Premier League title race could soon be academic.

City have picked up just six points from five matches since the verdict was announced in September. That’s nine fewer points than Mikel Arteta’s Gunners, four fewer than Tottenham, and fewer than the likes of Brighton, Bournemouth, Fulham and Everton.

Richard Lloyd Dies: Guitarist With Influential Punk Pioneers Television Was 75

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Richard Lloyd, whose intricate guitar work for the pioneering punk band Television was a fiery counterpart to the sinewy stylings of the band’s co-guitarist and singer Tom Verlaine, died today of the bile duct cancer called cholangiocarcinoma at his home in Chattanooga, Tenn. He was 75.

His death was reported by his family to The New York Times. Lloyd had been diagnosed with the cancer in March.

With his Television bandmates Verlaine, bassist Richard Hell (later replaced by Fred Smith) and drummer Billy Ficca, Lloyd was among the innovative, even revolutionary, New York bands in the mid-1970s to establish the East Village dive bar CBGB as the epicenter of a new downtown music scene. Along with Patti Smith and the Ramones, Television led the way in creating and developing a new sound – defined later (and loosely) as punk – that was often as literate and playful as it was sonically powerful. Related Stories News Paul Howard Dies: Son Of Three Stooges' Moe Howard, Keeper Of The Flame Was 91 News Kimmarie Johnson Dies: 'Beverly Hills, 90210' & 'Dude, Where's My Car?' Actor Was 50

Formed in 1973, Television grew out of the teenage friendship of two Delaware boys, Thomas Miller and Richard Meyers, who by the early ’70s had changed their names to, respectively, Tom Verlaine and Richard Hell and set about developing the spiky guitar sound and keening vocal style (courtesy Verlaine) that would reach its apotheosis in the band’s 1977 masterpiece Marquee Moon. Watch on Deadline

By the time of their 1974 debut at CBGB, Verlaine and Hell had recruited Lloyd to be the group’s second guitarist, a move that gave Television its signature and distinctive dueling Strat-versus-Jazzmaster sound. Unlike the more power-chord-heavy guitar bombast of other bands on the scene, Television specialized in complicated, even byzantine interplay between the six-stringed instruments. And like Patti Smith, Verlaine sang lyrics that signified a devotion to poetry as surely as did his chosen name.

Born Richard John Alt on Oct. 25, 1951, in Pittsburgh, Lloyd grew up in Manhattan’s Greenwich Village and began playing guitar before he hit his teens. He became a regular in the audience of the famed Fillmore East rock palace, mesmerized by the music of The Grateful Dead and other bands of the era. He would be forthcoming about working as a male hustler to support himself during the lean years.

With an early residency at CBGB, Television quickly won over audiences as well as fellow musicians like Patti Smith, Lou Reed and David Bowie, who had caught the band during a trip to New York and set about spreading the word to anyone who would listen. Bowie would later record the group’s song “Kingdom Come” for his 1980 Scary Monsters album

The band’s initial line-up didn’t last long, though, as the old friends Verlaine and Hell began to bicker over everything from their music to the way it was performed on stage. Hell quit in 1975 and formed a sort of punk supergroup called The Heartbreakers with ex-New York Dolls members Johnny Thunders and Jerry Nolan. He eventually left that group to form Richard Hell and the Voidoids.

Hell was replaced in Television by ex-Blondie bassist Fred Smith (not to be confused with Patti Smith’s husband Fred “Sonic” Smith).

Despite its quick success, Television called it quits in 1978, in part because of Lloyd’s increasingly dire heroin addiction and mental health issues. Lloyd would release nine solo albums after Television, and he and Verlaine, despite a prickly relationship, occasionally reunited the band. Neither guitarist would ever match the success or influence of Television in its genre-building heyday.

Lloyd is survived by wife Sheila O’Keefe Lloyd; son Dylan; and brother Eric Lloyd.

Mortgage rates fell and demand steadied as inventory dipped

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Inventory declined week over week to 902,740 as mortgage rates eased for 3 straight weeks. With spreads at 2.27%, the path to sub-7% mortgage rates can happen with lower bond yields or better mortgage spreads.  AI Summary

Mortgage rates fell, demand stabilized and inventory had a noticeable decline last week. But are the changes in demand and inventory data about seasonality, or has the housing market changed with the recent mortgage rate decline? Let’s take a look at the weekend tracker and see what the data is telling us with rates falling the last three weeks.

In the 2026 HousingWire forecast, I anticipated the following ranges:

What a crazy time in the bond market recently: the key level of 5.35% has not only held since early October, but the reversal has been truly nice to watch. The midterm elections, which changed the control of the House and Senate, have added another variable into the mortgage rate equation. I discussed what Congress can do to help lower mortgage rates on this episode of the HousingWire Daily podcast with Editor in Chief Sarah Wheeler, but for now the head-and-shoulders pattern of the 10-year yield is still somewhat intact, though a fight for it to break lower is happening.

I wouldn’t get too excited about mortgage rates until the 10-year yield can break under 4.70% and have follow-through bond buying, but mortgage rates have fallen and demand has stabilized over the last few weeks.

Mortgage spreads have worsened recently, but they’re still trending near multiyear lows. Traditionally, when mortgage rates escalate with the 10-year yield and then make a big reversal — like we just had from a 5.35% 10-year yield top that dropped down toward 4.82% —  spreads do get worse to try to compress mortgage rate volatility.That said, if mortgage spreads weren’t near multiyear lows right now, we’d have no chance of getting mortgage rates below 7.25%, and demand wouldn’t have stabilized recently. Now that spreads have moved up from this year’s lows, we can clearly see that mortgage rates would be sub-7% if spreads return to that level.

Historically, mortgage spreads have ranged from 1.60% to 1.80%. Last week, spreads rose to 2.27% from 2.23% a week earlier.

Let’s compare last week’s mortgage rates to where they would have been at the best spread levels this year and the worst levels of this cycle.

Our pending home sales data provides a week-to-week perspective, though holidays and short-term fluctuations can affect results. This weekly pending sales data typically takes 30-60 days to reflect in sales data.

The key for the last three weeks: lower rates, even above 7%, have stabilized demand, as our weekly pending sales have shown. Now, this isn’t growth, just stabilization from a noticeable slowdown when mortgage rates got toward 7.60%. The last three years and 10 months show that housing demand grows when mortgage rates fall below 6.64% and head toward 6%; above 7%, we don’t have much growth data beyond seasonal demand. Last year at this time, mortgage rates were headed toward 6%, so the year-over-year comps will be hard for some time.

Here are the pending sales for last week over the last two years:

Purchase application data, which looks out 30-90 days, has softened as mortgage rates rose above 6.64% and are now above 7%. We had a small decline week to week; I can see the stabilization here as well and the year-over-year comps will also be very difficult here, as last year at this time rates were heading toward 6%. Earlier in the year, when rates were lower, pretty much every week this year showed growth.

Here are the stats on purchase apps so far in 2026:

Housing inventory growth has been very tame this year, with some weeks being down year over year. However, when mortgage rates got above 6.64% and then headed toward 7.60%, we did see some growth pick up. Also, rates were lower last year so demand was heading toward a nine-month high in December, which means it the comps will be easier to show growth in inventory.

Inventory growth is running at 7.18% even with easier comps.

New listings are in their typical seasonal decline; 2026 has been the healthiest new-listings year since 2022, with over 80,000 new listings per week a few times this year. The concern I had was that if higher rates cause sellers to stop listing their homes — as they did in the second half of 2022 — that would hurt supply and demand. However, that hasn’t happened in any meaningful way. Most home sellers are buyers, and now that rates have fallen a smidge, it looks like we can end the year on a positive note for 2026 for new listings.

In a normal environment, new listings range from 80,000 to 100,000 per week during peak periods. For context, during the housing bubble years, new listings ranged from 250,000 to 400,000 per week for several years.Here is last week’s new listings data for the past two years:

Typically, about one-third of homes see price reductions before they sell, reflecting the housing market’s dynamic nature. Overall, price-cut percentages this year have been lower than last year. This held true until rates moved above 6.64% and then above 7%; demand fell, and the year-over-year price-cut percentage rose 1%.In my 2026 home-price forecast, I called for a national decline of -0.62% for the year. Home-price growth really isn’t going anywhere this year, and my -0.62% forecast may be hard to achieve, since most home-price indexes show growth between 1% and 2%. However, with higher rates and a rising price-cut percentage, we will be closer to the forecast. Clearly, if rates hadn’t risen above 6.75%, pricing data wouldn’t have shown the recent slowdown.

You want to keep an eye on how AI stocks trade this week, in particular CoreWeave’s stock price and how the 10-year yield reacts. For now, there’s an interesting correlation between the two, but I expect it to fade over time.

It’s inflation week again, with both CPI and PPI reports. Over the next 12 months, if the Iran conflict ends and the trade war doesn’t get worse, the question is whether bond traders will put more weight on those two factors than on the current inflation reports, which could improve in the future. This would be a huge positive for mortgage rates, but still, it will take a lot of work to end the trade war and the conflict. Existing home sales will come out and it won’t be pretty. We also have a lot of Fed speeches this week and it’s critical now to see what they say and how the bond market reacts to their talking points. Also, with the midterms over, I will be watching whether any of them start to talk about the end of the conflict and trade war being a positive for their future outlook.

Because of Okafor: Nobody is Talking About How Bahoya's Leeds Career is Over

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Noah Okafor was the match-winner for Leeds United as they recorded a much-needed 1-0 victory over Tottenham on Saturday.

Okafor, 26, struck less than two minutes after coming on as the Whites earned a first win in five matches and moved back into the Premier League top-half.

The Switzerland international, who missed the 2-0 defeat at Bournemouth last time out with an adductor issue, was introduced as a 77th minute substitute at Elland Road.

He replaced Lukas Nmecha, who started as the left forward, looking to make an impact and help his side find a breakthrough after finding the Tottenham defence a hard nut to crack.

And just 97 seconds after his introduction, Okafor found the net. He combined with Harry Wilson inside the left channel before finding some space and slamming the ball past Antonin Kinksy.

It was a piece of quality and invention that was missing from Leeds, and there is no doubt that Okafor should be starting week-in-week out when fit.

He is someone that can create a chance out of nowhere and is Leeds' most potent attacker, barring Dominic Calvert-Lewin, of course. Lukas Nmecha worked hard and is a good back-up option, but Okafor, who scored eight goals last season, is the best Farke has in that position.

With Okafor coming back in and showing that he should be in Farke's XI, Jean-Matteo Bahoya's Leeds career could now be over. The Frenchman, 21, who joined the Whites on loan from Eintracht Frankfurt in the summer, was left out of the 20-man squad to face Tottenham.

But Bahoya has played just 11 minutes for Leeds since his arrival, making three appearances in the process. He came on as a late substitute in the defeat at Bournemouth, but was unable to impact the game as the Whites were beaten at the Vitality Stadium.

There is no doubt Bahoya has talent, having showed that in glimpses at Frankfurt, but he has been unable to show that since he moved to Leeds. He might not be showing enough in training, but what is clear is that Farke isn't having him right now.

With Leeds scoring just once in three matches prior to the win against Tottenham, it did feel as though Farke should have been using Bahoya more. But the German provided a 10-minute explanation last month on the reason the forward isn't getting more minutes.

Farke outlined the fact he is a "young lad still getting used to the Premier League" but reiterated he does "love" Bahoya and that he is going to get his chance.

At this stage, it doesn't look promising for Bahoya, however. While Leeds were struggling in attack, he wasn't called up and that felt like the perfect time for him to get more game time.

For now, Bahoya is going to have to bide his time, but his Leeds career might be over before it even got started, especially if Frankfurt consider recalling him in January.

Okafor should start for Leeds on the left-hand side with Nmecha as back-up and Bahoya down as third-choice, though Brenden Aaronson could even be in front of the ex-Angers man.

Sony delays PlayStation's Pulse Elevate speakers indefinitely, less than a week before launch

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sony has delayed its Pulse Elevate wireless speakers indefinitely, less than a week before they were due to launch.

The news came in a 6th November update to Sony’s original announcement on the PlayStation Blog, first spotted by IGN. Quality testing has turned up a "production issue", Sony said, and there's no new release date yet.

"As part of our ongoing quality testing of Pulse Elevate speakers, we have identified a production issue with the product and have made the decision to delay the launch," the statement reads. "The additional time will allow us to ensure the product will deliver the experience we intended for our players. We apologise for the delay and appreciate your patience and understanding. We look forward to bringing Pulse Elevate to players soon."

The speakers were set to go on sale worldwide on 12th November, and pre-orders opened in September. It's unclear what, exactly, happens to those orders, but we'll update as and when we find out more.

Sony revealed Pulse Elevate in September 2025 as its first wireless speakers for desktop gaming. They sit alongside the Pulse Elite headset and Pulse Explore earbuds, and cost £199.99 ($219.99).

They connect to PS5, PC, Mac, and PlayStation Portal via PlayStation Link, and can pair with a phone over Bluetooth at the same time. There's also a microphone in the right speaker with what Sony calls "AI-enhanced noise rejection".

Midnight Black was due to be available everywhere, while the White version was limited to a handful of countries, including the UK.

Alongside the Pulse Elevate speakers, Sony revealed a range of hardware in September 2025, including a 27-inch gaming monitor that's coming only to the US and Japan.

Mikel Arteta identifies two missing elements that can raise Arsenal’s level

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Arsenal 1-0 Hull: The Gunners’ manager called for more pragmatism and chaos after Martin Odegaard’s goal secured a familier scoreline for Arsenal

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

After a familiar scoreline, Mikel Arteta has a new phrase.

The Arsenal manager used some variation on “insist” several times throughout his press conference for the win over Hull City, after his team had almost been left standing at the door.

Martin Odegaard instead offered a captain’s goal, for a third 1-0 win in four in the league. Like many of those, it could have been worse, but could have been better – especially in terms of finishing.

Arsenal currently always feel one setback away from a big discussion about which forward they need to sign in January. They are obviously monitoring a few options, and are expected to do some business, but Arteta clearly doesn’t want his players thinking like that.

When asked about what was actually a sixth win by one goal out of 10 Premier League games so far, he said they “need to be more efficient”.

It could be argued that such a return is the height of efficiency, and that Arsenal could have been further ahead had they benefited from that debated penalty decision – but you know what he meant, especially with regard to finishing.

Arteta lamented how his team had two further one-on-ones in the game, only to waste them. He said it was like they “are trying to score the perfect goal”.

That’s another familiar refrain with this club, if from a different era to the 1-0s. Arteta’s desired solution is to be “more pragmatic”, that they “need to be more chaotic”.

That’s undeniably true, but the manager being the one to say this poses some questions of his own.

Another phrase that Arteta used was “volume”. That, when you face a defence as well structured as Hull City, you need to be “generating volume”. In other words, numbers, a critical mass of chances where you eventually score – which is pretty much what they did. Insistence.

It’s been discussed on these pages before that Arteta is a huge “probabilities guy”, where he sees almost every game as an equation. If Arsenal consistently generate an xG of 2 or above and limit the opposition to 0.5, they are likely to win enough games to win the league.

A natural extension of this is an exactingly calibrated team, where Hull City players – like so many others – were left praising “the shape”.

But is that mentality something that encourages “pragmatism” or, as Arteta elaborated, “chaos”? Other descriptions for that are sparkle, ingenuity… magic.

That’s hard to find sufficient space for in a system that demands a lot of energy devoted to pressing and structure.

Odegaard, the match-winner, is almost the ultimate example. The playmaker is capable of some of the most ingenious and exquisite touches in the league, but it does not feel like he is as characterised by that as much any more.

When you ask people at Arsenal about him, in fact, they praise how he understands Arteta’s pressing better than anyone else. This is why he is captain and what they were said to really miss last season when he was out. Odegaard leads the press. When he goes, everyone immediately knows when and where to follow.

An irony is that Noni Madueke is one of the most chaotic players in the league – in a good way. He is a rare attacker that takes people on. As is the case when you see his and Christos Tzolis’s names on the teamsheet, you don’t quite know what you’re going to get in terms of performance level. Club insiders say it could be anything between a two and an eight out of 10.

The most frustrating thing about Madueke, in fact, is that he usually ends such runs with a predictable cross rather than real variation. He doesn’t shoot enough, which touches on Arteta’s point about trying to score “a perfect goal”.

All of this came together for a moment that should have sealed the win.

Arsenal’s attack put together a brilliant flowing move at 1-0, to leave Madueke through for one of those one-on-ones. It was close to perfect. Until, with just Konstantinos Tzolakis to beat, Madueke tried to roll the ball past him only to get his feet wrong.

While the forward clearly has potential, the point of this is that Arsenal still feel at least one attacker – but arguably two – away from really being at the level they want to be.

It should be stressed that their current level isn’t exactly much of a problem. They’ve won nine out of 10 in the league. They’re brilliantly efficient in everything outside the scoring.

Even this match has to be put in the context of a tough match against a very respectable Hull City side. They’re so well structured you can see them being this season’s Sunderland.

Arsenal were known to be impressed afterwards, with Arteta especially respectful. His team still get through, and you can see them being convincingly ahead by Christmas.

But the league isn’t the level anymore. It’s about beating Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League, about being European champions as well as English champions.

That is something they really have to insist on.

Time between pregnancies may shape how fast mothers age

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Each pregnancy adds biological age that takes months to come off, and birth spacing may decide how much a mother recovers before the next one.

Tests suggest that biological age rises during pregnancy and falls again in the months after birth. Researchers are now asking whether the time between pregnancies affects how fully those changes reverse.

Two Harvard researchers propose that the time a woman gets between pregnancies, not only how many she has, may shape how fast she ages for decades.

If the next pregnancy begins before that recovery is finished, the extra age may not be fully reversed, the authors wrote. The effect could then build with each closely spaced birth.

Stephen Albright and Jesse Poganik of Harvard Medical School and Brigham and Women’s Hospital laid out the idea in a review article. It pulls together studies from Europe, the United States, Israel and the Philippines.

The result is a hypothesis with testable predictions attached, not a new trial, and Poganik said the changes it describes don’t necessarily amount to damage.

“Our previous research has found that biological age transiently rises during pregnancy, peaking around the third trimester or at the time of delivery,” said Poganik, the corresponding author.

“If we accept the idea that these short-term changes in biological age occur, the question is how we can relate those changes to the overall trajectory of aging over the course of a lifetime.”

Biological age is an estimate of how much age-related change a body’s cells and tissues have accumulated, separate from the years on a birth certificate.

One way to estimate it is to read chemical tags on DNA that shift in a predictable pattern as people get older. Researchers call those readouts epigenetic clocks.

Run those clocks on blood drawn through a pregnancy and the number climbs as the pregnancy goes on, then partly falls after the birth.

Women on three continents show the same pattern, and so do mice. The authors wrote that this level of agreement is uncommon in their field.

The changes behind the number are physical. Blood volume rises by about half.

Cortisol, a stress hormone, climbs through pregnancy, and estrogen runs at roughly 100 times its usual level. The body also builds a new organ, the placenta, from nothing.

A detailed picture of the recovery comes from Israel, where researchers analyzed 76 routine lab tests from more than 300,000 pregnancies.

About half of those markers needed anywhere from three months to a year to return to where they had been before the pregnancy.

An aging clock built from those same lab results showed a dip before a large rise. In the first trimester a woman’s results looked about five years younger than her age.

From there they rose by about 20 years toward delivery, and it took roughly a year for them to recover.

Not every system recovers on the same schedule. Among more than 200,000 people, the reading from a clock built from wearable pulse sensors rose by about 3.5 years during pregnancy, then declined only slowly after birth.

Brain scans of first-time mothers showed gray matter volume falling across nearly all of the cortex during pregnancy and only partly recovering afterward.

The most direct evidence comes from a 2026 study that followed 130 women from pregnancy through nearly four years after the birth.

Among women who became pregnant again, the decline in their measured pace of aging was interrupted, and two other aging measures moved in the same direction.

Albright and Poganik call the time between a birth and the next conception the recovery window, and their hypothesis is simple to state.

Each pregnancy adds some biological age, and given enough time the body can undo at least part of it.

Under their hypothesis, another pregnancy could add to any biological-age increase that has not yet reversed.

Two women with the same number of children could therefore end up aging at different rates, depending on how far apart the births were.

The authors wrote that this is their central prediction, and it hasn’t been directly tested. No study has yet measured the spacing of pregnancies alongside biological age tracked for decades afterward.

Mothers of many children tend to die earlier in some populations and not in others, and the reasons remain unclear.

In the Finnish Twin Cohort, the risk of dying was highest among women with no births and women with five or more, and lowest among women with two or three.

Among adoptive parents, death rates declined as the number of adopted children increased.

A study of 2,356 women aged 35 to 74 linked more births to small increases in epigenetic age, measured well after their last pregnancy.

In 1,735 young women in the Philippines, with an average age of about 22, each additional pregnancy was linked to roughly two to three months of extra aging.

Spacing could explain why the count alone gives such mixed results, the authors wrote. Where women with many children tend to space them well, the count would carry little cost.

“It’s possible that this could change the overall trajectory of aging,” Poganik said.

“We know that some level of parity, or carrying a pregnancy to term, is actually beneficial to a person’s lifespan.”

The World Health Organization has recommended since 2005 that women wait at least 24 months after a live birth before conceiving again.

The advice rests on evidence linking short gaps to more preterm and low-weight births.

One review of 129 studies and nearly 47 million pregnancies linked gaps of 18 to 23 months to the best outcomes for babies. Gaps under six months were most consistently linked to harm for both baby and mother.

When a new pregnancy began six months after a birth, instead of 18 months, only women 35 and older faced a substantially higher chance of dying or becoming severely ill.

The age pattern fits their idea, Albright and Poganik wrote, because an older mother starts the recovery period biologically older, with less reserve.

Some factors were associated with differences in recovery.

Among about 120 women in one cohort, those who breastfed exclusively had a lower biological age three months after birth, whatever their body mass index.

A higher body mass index before pregnancy predicted a higher biological age afterward.

Sleep loss and chronic stress are suspected of slowing recovery too, though the authors found little or no research on either after birth.

Not every population fits. Tsimane women, who farm and forage for a living, have many children and little spare food.

Over a 10-year study their nutrition and immune markers largely held up however many children they had.

But those measures weren’t combined into a biological age, the authors wrote, so the cost they propose can’t be ruled out there.

Whether the extra age is wear, or the body remodeling itself for a demanding job, isn’t settled either. Clocks designed to tell the two apart are one way to find out.

Poganik said the work has a nearer-term purpose as well.

“What motivates me on a more near-term basis is addressing current clinical challenges, including the huge maternal mortality crisis in the United States compared with other industrialized countries,” he said.

“Any insights we can gain that can improve the health of mothers makes this a worthwhile research endeavor.”

The question underneath all of it is whether an incomplete recovery leaves a mother permanently older on the clocks, or whether the deficit fades given enough time.

Answering it means measuring biological age through several pregnancies, then following those women for 10 to 20 years, and no cohort has done that.

Until then, the 24-month guidance rests on pregnancy outcomes. Poganik said any advice based on a mother’s biological age would have to wait for that longer follow-up.

The review was published in the journal Trends in Endocrinology & Metabolism.

Roma director Tony D’Amico admits the defeat to Manchester United was ‘a learning experience’ for them, confirming the need to sign ‘a forward who can do damage on the counter-attack’ when leading.

The match with Sassuolo kicks off at the Stadio Olimpico in Rome at 19.45 GMT (20.45 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s four Serie A games on the Liveblog.

The Giallorossi are still unbeaten this season in Serie A, but have twice thrown away massive leads in the Champions League for defeats to Real Madrid and now Manchester United.

That Old Trafford collapse from 2-0 up to lose 3-2 confirmed this is a recurring problem, so this evening the pressure is on to get a result against struggling Sassuolo.

“It is a problem we realised towards the end of the transfer session, that we lack a wide forward who has pace and can do damage in those situations on the counter-attack,” D’Amico told Sky Sport Italia.

“We will try to correct that when the January window comes around, so will try to give the coach this extra characteristic that we don’t currently have.”

It has been said that Roma lack experience in the Champions League and that cost them when their heads dropped in those situations.

“What I saw in both the games against Manchester United and Real Madrid was a great Roma side that imposes its style of play on the strongest opponents. There are going to be moments of difficulty in fixtures like this.

“I don’t think enough has been said, partly because we don’t like to court controversy, but the penalty for Manchester United was more than generous. So we did have a moment of difficulty, but that penalty sparked the comeback.

“As I said after the Real Madrid defeat, these are learning experiences for a club that has not been in the Champions League for seven years, it also teaches us what resources we still need to give to the coach in order to continue this progress.”

“We speak with all the international coaches, Koné returned to training this week, we are pleased he is back and it gives us the chance to rest someone who played nine games in 28 days.”

£5m Wolves man faces fresh dilemma: Cardiff City & Norwich City must act

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The recruitment team at Wolves will already have one eye on the January transfer window as they look to strengthen Cesar Peixoto’s squad to help their promotion push.

It seems inevitable that Wanderers will try to bring in a new striker, but, aside from the lack of depth up top, the Portuguese head coach will be pleased with the overall state of the squad.

However, one problem facing Wolves is the size of the squad, as they are already at the 25-man limit. Therefore, to secure new additions, players will have to leave.

And, one man who may be open to a move is Tommy Doyle, with the midfielder having only played 281 minutes in the Championship this season heading out of the midweek fixtures.

As well as that, Peixoto doesn’t seem to know Doyle’s best position, as he had operated higher up the pitch before coming on as a number six in the recent win over Bristol City.

So, the £5m signing may feel he is at a stage in his career where he can play regularly and nail down a permanent home.

With that in mind, we look at TWO Championship clubs who should be targeting the 25-year-old in the New Year…

The Bluebirds have had some fantastic moments back in the Championship under the guidance of Brian Barry-Murphy, who has implemented a clear style of play with the Welsh side.

Yet, the one major problem Cardiff have right now is a lack of depth, and, in truth, they could do with strengthening in various areas in the January transfer window.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

A new midfielder would certainly be welcome, even if it isn’t a priority, and Doyle is ideally suited to the way that Barry-Murphy wants to play, as he is excellent on the ball.

The Cardiff fans got to see Doyle’s qualities during a loan spell in the 2021/22 campaign, but he has matured a lot as a player since then, and he could now slot in as an important figure in the XI.

Again, he should be able to flourish in a possession-based approach, as Doyle can help control the tempo, and he would get the ball forward quickly. Plus, his mentality and experience at this level should help a relatively young Bluebirds squad.

After a slow start to the season, Norwich look to have finally turned a corner, and there will be a belief that they can still win promotion this season, which was always the aim under Philippe Clement.

But, the first few months of the season have shown that Norwich are still lacking depth, even if they have been very unfortunate with injuries.

With Kenny McLean missing too many games through injury, Norwich are in need of a new midfielder who can hold the ball, move it quickly, and help control games - which is what Doyle is all about.

Should Wolves let Tommy Doyle leave the club in January? Let us know in the comments below.

It remains to be seen whether Wolves would be willing to sell to the Canaries, as they could be promotion rivals, but it’s certainly something that Norwich should explore.

That’s because, on paper, it could be a great move for Doyle and Norwich, and he is the sort of addition that Clement should look to make to ensure his group can last the distance in the promotion race this season.
